# Launch checklist

Kept by Lane F (the Desk). Docs-only commits, each pushed by itself on top of origin/main. Every row carries an owner,
a state and a proof. States: building, ready, pushing, live, checking, blocked, decision (Sam's call), unknown (asked,
not yet answered). "Proof" names where the evidence sits (a lane-notes section, a script, a screenshot folder); a
row with no proof is not done whatever its state says.

Last updated: 2026-10-20T12:11Z (Lane F; Lane C's 12:15 prePush carries trim_choice, photo widths and Lane B's dry-run commit).

## 0cs. PUSH PROTOCOL LINE (Lane B, Oct 20 2026, item 11, Lane F's order)

After any ENGINE_VERSION bump: Sam runs the rollout cache workflow ("Rebuild VIN rollout cache", from docs/vin-rollout-workflow.yml once copied to .github/workflows/vin-rollout.yml), then Lane E reads sitemap-vins.xml for 301s. Until it runs, the VIN sitemap keeps the names of the last 07:11 UTC nightly, so a page whose car name changed answers its old sitemap address with a 301. The workflow makes no OldCarsData calls and takes a few minutes.

## 0cq. Sam 19:5xZ Oct 19: THE BUY REDESIGN (the order verbatim in docs/lane-notes.md; the mockup at ~/Desktop/goasksam-proofs/handoff/buy-redesign-mockup.png; Sam's written brief was not on disk, so the order is the working spec)

Build it and ship it LIVE stage by stage, no preview or screenshot step (Sam reviews the live Buy page himself). Principle: cars first, Sam when useful, conversation always available. OWNERS: Lane A builds the page and card visuals (reachable only through lane-notes); Lane C integrates the shared card, the pinned composer and EVERY push; Lane E owns the Buy behaviour (narrowing question, suggestions, keep-warm) and states the money-label truth per source by 21:00Z; Lane B only if an engine line or field is missing. DEFAULTS (change only if Sam says): (1) every figure, range, count, reason line and placing word comes from the one engine's answer for the car on that card (the mockup text is placeholder; its 1997 Targa card showing a range of 997 Turbo Coupe sales is a different car); (2) thin cards print the engine's own line for the card's reason code (first sentence only until the card lists the sales; the Buy rows are live, so the full line shows where rows are listed); (3) range wording stays as the shared card prints it ('Half of these sold in this range.' and the placing word), layout only: number first, line under it, then the placing word; (4) money labels: BID only for a provably live feed, LAST BID SEEN with the time otherwise, ASKING for a classified, BUY NOW for a fixed price, never a bare number; (5) the Keep looking task once after the last card (KEEP LOOKING, 'Nothing else right now.', 'Have Sam keep looking for {the current search} ->', 'Sam will email you when another matching car goes live.'), inheriting the current search; the one-task beta message and the sign-in requirement stay; (6) no mic icon and no photo icon until they work; (7) the history popup keeps the Oct 15 rule ('This car has sold before' only when a prior listing of that VIN actually sold, otherwise 'listed before'; 'Back again with 4,000 more miles.' a fixed template from the two sales' mileage, never model-written; the earlier sale not also written in the Sam paragraph); (8) inline Market Check shows the engine's own list, newest first, compact, under the card's row, marks the active card, 'Open full Market Check ->'. STAGE 1 CARDS (subtractive): photo, labelled money number, car, mileage/location/spec, the Sam block (small uppercase green SAM, thin green vertical rule, regular serif, no italics, subtle tone shift, no boxes, bubbles or avatar), actions (View auction ->, Market Check); remove the visible VIN pill and 'Have Sam keep looking' from every card; 'See its earlier sale' or 'See its history' opens the popup (bottom sheet on mobile); keep three-across and large photos. STAGE 2 SEARCH STATE: no second permanent search box; a compact current-search line with removable constraint chips (Porsche x, Black x, Manual x, <50k miles x), removal applies at once, the request said once only (remove 'Your search:', 'Here's what's for sale', the repeated '7 live ... Which model?'), no permanent Near me, Any colour, Any gearbox or Include cars abroad pills; + New search stays in the rail. STAGE 3 THE COMPOSER (built on the pinned question bar already live): placeholder 'Tell Sam what to change, or ask about any car...'; at most three context-aware suggestions above it behaving exactly as if typed; refinements and questions about the shown cars through the existing Buy chat and its output guard ('I can't tell that from these listings' when it cannot); no chat bubbles, sparkle icons, avatar, typing animation or transcript; narrowing: 1 to 8 cars no question (a quiet 'Want to narrow it?'), 9 to 25 optional light refinement, 25 and over one high-value question, never before the cars; model, generation, body kept separate. STAGE 4 INLINE MARKET CHECK AND MOBILE. GUARDRAILS: warm cream, dark ink, deep green, editorial serif, clean sans, large photography, fine rules, no blue; no new public wording beyond the brief; no count of sources, no 'our data', no 'AI' label, no other tools; the page computes nothing; the left rail stays. SHIP: each stage under one PUSH LOCK, full prePush alone in a named gap, one stage at a time; live checks at 1440 and 390 in the empty, answered, thin, refused and still-reading states; Buy's cold-read speed must not regress; revert at once on any change to a count, a range or a line, a card showing a bare money number, a card losing its photo or actions, or a broken search. LAUNCH ORDER unchanged: Market Check and Tasks first, Buy at stage 2. The partner demo stays HELD. LANE F SCHEDULE: Stage 1 build now; Stage 1 FULL prePush alone tentatively 22:35:00Z (after the 21:35Z bundle, its after-reads 21:50 and 22:05Z and Lane E's 22:20Z audit); each later stage in a gap named after the previous one is live and clean.

## 0cf. Sam 06:5xZ Oct 19: STANDING AUTHORITY while Sam sleeps (about eight hours from now; routed to every lane)

Sam: make decisions based on what the launch needs; take the most reasonable reading, state it in the decision log, keep going; a quiet order never waits on Sam. (1) ORDER OF DECIDING: (a) correctness over speed ("better nothing than a fake number"); (b) one engine; (c) the launch gates as ranked in c93f1d10; (d) reversible over irreversible; (e) no spend; (f) nothing public changes beyond approved pushes. (2) F MAY DECIDE AND ORDER: anything already approved in this thread; the order and timing of gaps; one archive load at a time; reassigning a stuck order (Lane C is the fallback integrator; Lane A is back); fixing defects that follow the approved rules; pushes under the push protocol with the full prePush green, then live checks, then an immediate revert on any failure. PRIORITY QUEUE: (1) e65 push, after-reads with the 27-row before and after, warm, keep-warm list, re-read the demo cars; (2) B2: re-pins and golden pass on e65, article rows re-read (all fifteen, with and without asOf 2026-10-16), extent classification rerun, reconciliation; (3) Lane C: thin-wording push and C1 and C2 to C5 crawler safeguards when a quiet window allows; (4) Lane E: E9, the --rows health reads, then the SEO audit; (5) Lane D: Sell line and the Task email read (Lane C fallback at the 60 minute mark); (6) Lane A: catch-up, layout work toward the mockup; (7) Lane B: the keepHouse side-by-side for the ten cars, plain text for Sam. (3) F MUST NOT DECIDE, parks for Sam: any rule that changes what a range says (market rule, house-sale drop, counting non-dollar sales, thin rule, the modified rule beyond what Sam approved); any new public wording, title, H1, sitemap or link change; any visible redesign of a public page (Lane A builds and tests it, publishes only to a preview path or branch, leaves screenshots for Sam); launch switches (launch stage, LAUNCH_BUSINESS, CURTAIN_SEALED, the Supabase hook, the firewall publish); any secret rotation; any spend (no re-ingest, no OldCarsData requests); any SQL or migration on Supabase, any deletion of data; any email, message or post to anyone (the note to Cameron stays with Sam; the demo address and code go to no one); making the partner demo reachable by anyone; the article text or LinkedIn lines (the Desk drafts them after the re-reads). (4) SECURITY: no secrets in chat, files or logs. (5) IF A PUSH GOES WRONG: revert at once with the ready rollback, record the reason, stop that lane, tell the other lanes, carry on with work that does not touch the same files; do not retry the same push more than once without a changed cause. (6) DECISION LOG: section "Decisions made while Sam slept" below. (7) IF THE APPROVED QUEUE RUNS OUT: prepare, do not improvise (clean tables ready for the Desk, keep-warm schedules healthy, re-read the demo cars after each engine bump, write the morning summary). (8) SAM'S FIRST REPORT WHEN HE WAKES, in this order: (a) what is live now (pushes with times), (b) decisions made, (c) what needs Sam, short list with a recommendation each, (d) the launch gates table updated, (e) the article rows: clean, held, void.

## 0by. Sam 05:0xZ Oct 19: rebuild and change-words decided, house drop side-by-side planned (forwarded word for word to Lane B)

1. REBUILD: a rebuild of the car's own engine in stock specification is maintenance and NEVER sets a sale aside; a rebuild to a different specification (stroker, built engine, forged internals, larger displacement) does; stated in the rule text and the spot-check header. 2. CHANGE WORDS: a light software tune or a bolt-on on a stock car never sets a sale aside by itself; a stage-numbered tune (stage 1 and up), a big or custom tune with added boost or a bigger turbo, a built engine, an engine swap, a conversion kit or a widebody conversion does; the final words list goes at the top of the spot-check lists in plain words. 3. DETERMINISTIC RULE, narrowest: only inside carIdentity (api/_historyData.js): if the record's OWN stored model column (the archive's model for that VIN; 'Suburban' for 1GNFK16K2PJ402195) is a known model of that make AND appears in the title AND differs from the resolver's pick, the stored model wins and the resolver's pick becomes the trim where it adds to it; when the stored model is missing, unknown or not in the title, today's behaviour stands; the archive column is data, so the answer is the same with and without the tables. PREVIEW: the golden, demo and article cars are TYPED searches, which never pass through carIdentity, so none can move. THE SPREAD RULE (lib/onebox.js, the range step): ratio = the 90th-percentile sale over the 10th-percentile sale (max over min under 6 sales); an answer is REFUSED as too spread out when (a) ratio > 6, or (b) the pool spans 15 or more model years AND holds 2 or more variant tokens AND ratio > 2.5, or (c) a blend nameplate is forced varied (the bare '250 GT' kind). Separately wideSpread = true when the printed middle-half range's top is more than 2 times its bottom; that flag only puts a body (or trim) question first and never refuses on its own. THE 2006 FERRARI F430 SPIDER (16 sales, all 2006, sold Dec 2025 to Oct 2026): ratio p90/p10 = $485,000 / $143,000 = 3.39, year span 0, so NOT refused under (a) or (b): consistent with the rule; the printed range $179,000 to $392,000 is 2.19 times (wideSpread true, no body question to ask on a Spider-only pool). THE POOL MIXES GEARBOXES: 5 MANUAL ('6-Speed' in the title): $600,000, $485,000, $485,000, $440,000, $376,000; 11 F1 or gearbox not stated: $292,000, $220,000, $210,000, $208,000, $207,500, $200,000, $180,000, $177,000, $143,000, $123,000, $116,000. The two groups do not overlap (every manual sold above every F1 car), so the range sits across two markets and its top quarter is the manuals. No Scuderia, 16M or Challenge car is in it (47 '430 Scuderia' and 'Scuderia Spider 16M' sales and 5 F430 Challenge race cars excluded). The gearbox refinement exists and qualifies here (5 manual and 11 F1 clears the 5-and-5 split gate): 'Manual' re-runs the pool to the 5 manual sales (five sales: no range, the few-sales line). The card label '(Manual)' printing the BLENDED range suggests the card shows the listing's own gearbox but the market answer is the unrefined pool; which surface Sam saw decides the fix (a card-side refine, or a rule that splits on gearbox first). CAUSE: the hub key is built by joining slugify(make) + '-' + slugify(family) WITHOUT dropping an empty part (api/history.js:793 in the VIN-sitemap rollout; the same unfiltered join at api/history.js:356 for a car page's 'All ... results' link and at api/_historyData.js:351 for allHref); the sitemap's gate is `if (!g.make || !saneFamily(g.family))`, so a make that is NON-EMPTY but slugifies to '' passes (a dash, a symbol, or non-Latin text, since slugify keeps only a to z and 0 to 9); canonicalHub (_historyData.js:132) never blanks a make, so this car's stored make must be such a string, with the real name 'Ford Falcon Sprint V8' in its family; the result is a hub with an empty make part, a leading dash, 'Ford' duplicated into the family, and a hub title built from the family alone; the car-level slugs use .filter(Boolean) (carSlug, history.js:523 and 598) so they differ from the hub key. Whether the page answers 200 with a sensible title and canonical needs one page read. NARROWEST FIX: (1) in the rollout, gate on the SLUG, not the raw string: `if (!slugify(g.make) || !saneFamily(g.family))`, counted as no_proper_title, so a car with no usable make gets no hub and no sitemap entry; (2) the three unfiltered joins use .filter(Boolean), so no link carries a leading dash; (3) if the page already answers 200 with an indexable canonical, a 301 from the dashed slug to the trimmed one, or a 404 when the trimmed slug names no hub; the car's true make stays in its family, so naming it properly is a data fix for its stored make (report only). DECISION: Lane C builds (1) and (2) locally; one page read 21:16:00 to 21:18:00Z (status, title, canonical, robots); (3) waits for that read; NOT SHIPPED: a sitemap entry removal and link changes are parked for Sam under the standing authority.

- Lane B, 04:5xZ Oct 19, ITEM 5, THE HOUSE DROP, from code (no rule changed): "First, a CORRECTION to my earlier report: the drop is NOT only on the family path. WHAT IT DOES. In runOneBox (lib/onebox.js, the 'online' helper inside the trim-to-family ladder), every pool the main answer fetches goes through one step: when that pool holds three or more online sales, every house sale in it is removed before the range is built. When it holds fewer than three online sales, house sales stay. It applies on every rung of the main ladder: exact trim, trim over five years, family, family over five years. Thin mode is separate. It keeps house sales as receipts and counts them for the house steer, so a thin car shows its house sales. So a car with plenty of online sales gets a range from online sales only, and its house sales never show in that range, in its count or as set-aside sales. WHY IT EXISTS. The commit that added it (Sep 9 2026, the trim-to-family widening ladder) states no reason. From the code of the time, the likely reasons were: (a) House sales were then read with the buyer's premium still inside the price, because the premium back-out was inert at the time (a label against slug mismatch). Mixing them would have lifted every range. (b) The range reads the online auction market, where volume is, while house sales are spread across rooms and dates. - Lane B, 04:5xZ Oct 19, ITEM 2, THE MODIFIED RULE PROPOSAL (written work, no archive load, nothing built): rule: a sale is set aside as modified only when its listing DESCRIBES A CHANGE to the car's engine, drivetrain or body, or the car is a known outlaw build; three signals, any one sets it aside: (1) change language (swapped, swap, converted, conversion, aftermarket, installed, added, upgraded, tuned, tune, remapped, stage 1 to 3, big-bore, stroker, built, widebody, body kit, restomod) on an engine, induction or body part (engine, motor, turbocharger, supercharger, intercooler, block, heads, cams, gearbox, transmission, body, fenders, roll cage) in the seller's modifications text; (2) a modification descriptor in front of the year in the title (Bring a Trailer puts changes before the year, a factory feature after the model); (3) a builder or outlaw mark anywhere in the title (Singer, Reimagined, RUF, RWB, restomod, widebody, prerunner, trophy truck, conversion, custom, replica, tribute). Never sets aside alone: naming words (turbo, twin-turbo, supercharged, Kompressor, Tdi, intercooled, V8, a factory option), maintenance words (rebuilt, replaced, serviced, refreshed, overhauled, resealed), and suspension, wheels, exhaust and intake changes (a note). Guard instead of a hand list: when an engine or induction word appears in half or more of the sales of that exact model in the pool it is a feature of the model even in change language, so turbo on 911 Turbos or Tdi on Defenders never sets the model's own pool aside. - Lane B, 04:4xZ Oct 19, ITEM 2, the two rules, from code (no rule changed): "(a) NON-DOLLAR SALES. The engine has no currency rule as such. The rule is the MARKET rule, Sam's decision of Oct 15 2026: only sales in the United States and Canada enter any pool, range or count. Where it lives: marketCountry and inMarket in lib/_classify.js, applied in fetchQualifying in lib/onebox.js on every fetch, right after dedup. How the country is decided: the row's own country code first, then its state, then its city. Only when all three are missing does the currency decide: CAD reads as Canada (in), and any other non-dollar currency reads as abroad (out). The venue rule: Car & Classic, Collecting Cars, The Market and PistonHeads are always out. A Canadian sale stays in and is converted to dollars at the sale date's exchange rate. So a non-dollar sale is excluded because it happened abroad, not because of its currency. A euro sale in Germany is out; a CAD sale in Toronto is in. Why it exists: comparable sales must come from the market the visitor sells in. Before that decision, the UK, EU and Australian marketplaces were held out pending approval of UK routing. In the diagnosis read, these rows have their own class, 'abroad', with the reason 'outside the market (GB, by country_code)'. (b) HOUSE SALES. House sales ARE in the pool. They are receipts, read on the hammer with the buyer's premium backed out (lib/onebox.js priceDisplay and the outcome value). Two places treat them differently: THE THIN TEST (assessThin): only ONLINE sales with a photo count toward the eight (HT_ONLINE_MIN), so house sales never lift a car out of thin. HOUSE STEER fires when houses hold two-thirds or more of the pool. Why: the band needs volume, and house sales are sparse and spread across rooms. THE FAMILY OR HALO PATH (the 'online' helper in the halo-down read): when three or more online sales exist, house rows are dropped from that pool. This drop is silent. In the diagnosis read, thin answers carry the online count and the house count, but the silent family-path drop is NOT itemised. If Sam wants that itemised, it is a diagnosis-only change for e65 beside item 3. PER-CAR COUNTS for the fifteen weekend cars: one diagnosis read per car in the gap you name, reported as 'abroad by country' and 'house in the pool versus online' for each. Code 'few_sales' (thin, fewer than eight sales in all): 'Not enough sales of this exact car to give a reliable range. These are the sales we found.' No engine version bump (no answer changes), so no row recompute. - Lane B, 07:1xZ Oct 18: "(1) WITHDRAWN in lane-notes: the missing-index cause. The periodic CPU is not established, and I am not guessing. THE ONE PASTE (read-only, pg_stat_statements is enabled on Supabase projects by default and Sam used it earlier this week): select calls, round(total_exec_time::numeric) as total_ms, round(mean_exec_time::numeric, 1) as mean_ms, rows, left(query, 150) as query from pg_stat_statements where dbid = (select oid from pg_database where datname = current_database()) order by total_exec_time desc limit 10;  WHEN: run it twice, once at about :13 past the hour (just before a pull) and once at about :19 (just after the pull's three minutes), and compare the two grids: the statement whose total_ms GREW the most between them is what the pull episode spends its CPU on; the figures are cumulative since the last stats reset, so only the growth matters. WHAT EACH READING MEANS: a select on sales_archive with vin_norm in it whose mean_ms is in the hundreds or more = the planner is not using idx_sales_archive_vin_norm for that form (then I give an EXPLAIN); an insert or update on live_listings with 'on conflict' at the top = the pull's own upsert and status writes, which is index maintenance on live_listings (not the thirty on sales_archive, which the pull never rewrites); a select of vin_norm from sales_archive with 'in (' = liveStats' batches of eighty; a wide select from sales_archive with make, model and sale_date filters growing by thousands of rows = engine computes (the Tasks matching run at :05/:20/:35/:50 or a Market Check stale recompute) which are the known CPU cost per read; nothing user-visible growing while CPU is busy = autovacuum, confirmed by select * from pg_stat_progress_vacuum; and last_autovacuum in pg_stat_user_tables for sales_archive. If pg_stat_statements is not enabled the paste errors 'relation does not exist'; the next best read-only paste is select pid, now() - query_start as running_for, state, wait_event_type, left(query, 150) from pg_stat_activity where state <> 'idle' order by query_start; run at :16 during the pull. (2) FROM CODE, the pull's archive statements and their indexes: fillFinalPrices reads sales_archive where vin_norm = X and sale_date >= D order by sale_date desc limit 1 (equality on vin_norm: idx_sales_archive_vin_norm, then a sort of that car's few sales), and when that misses, where raw_record->>'url' = U limit 1 (the expression index sales_archive_raw_url_idx, same expression as PostgREST emits); liveStats reads sales_archive where vin_norm in (a list of 80) select vin_norm (the same btree, 80 probes) and auction_attempts where chassis_vin_norm in (80) (idx_attempts_vin). No function on vin_norm, no or= filter, no case-insensitive match, no large in-list; nothing in their form prevents index use, so the pull's lookups are not the CPU by construction, which is why the paste is needed. (3) THE PUSH, under Option A in the quiet gaps: the chain now waits for about four minutes past each quarter hour before the perf rows (retried once in the next gap if a run lands on an episode), the push, the warm, each after-read pass and the cold column. THE ONE PROMPT FOR SAM, in plain words: in Lane B's session (this one, job 412aab8f), allow the Bash command that starts the push chain. - Lane B, 05:5xZ Oct 18, the range's definition: "(1) WHAT THE RANGE IS: the low and high are the 25th and 75th percentiles of the included sales' prices, each rounded to the nearest $500 (lib/onebox.js r4Cluster), so the range is the middle half of the included sales, never a fence and never the lowest or highest sale. It is null on the thin, single and none tiers by design (under eight sales there is no range, product rule 24), though the thin tier carries its own thin.span on the sales it shows. Adding the two as numeric siblings on every tier that has included sales (lowestSaleN, highestSaleN, unrounded) is an addition-only change in one place, every product sees it; I can put it in e60 before the chain runs if Sam wants it, or in the next bump. (3) 'USUAL RANGE, NOT THE HIGHEST OR LOWEST': true by construction for every car with a range: the band is the middle half, so by definition about a quarter of the included sales sit below it and a quarter above it, and the lowest and highest included sales are outside it whenever the pool has more than four distinct prices (the only exception is a pool where the lowest or highest price repeats enough to reach the quartile, a tie, when the band's edge equals that price). The exact true sentence for every car is 'the middle half of the included sales landed between X and Y; about a quarter sold for less and a quarter for more', with the lowest and highest sale shown beside it from span."

- Lane B, 04:4xZ Oct 18, run-0 v2: "the run-0 bug is fixed and the run files are re-checked; no database touched. (1) THE BUG: Postgres has no max() aggregate for uuid, so the batch boundary is now the batch's last id taken in descending order (select the batch in id order, limit batch_rows, then its last row), exact, and the loop exits when the select returns no row. Fixed in run-0 v2, run-2 and run-2b (the three places it lived; run-1, run-4 and run-5 never had it). (2) THE RE-CHECK against Postgres 15 and 17 (Supabase's versions; I cannot read version() myself, so PART 1 of v2 is `select version();` for Sam to send back): clock_timestamp, extract(epoch from interval), get diagnostics row_count, pg_total_relation_size, pg_size_pretty, count(*) filter, tablesample system, rollup, create or replace procedure with COMMIT (PG11+) all exist on both. Two editor facts changed the files: (a) the Supabase editor does NOT show RAISE NOTICE output, so every per-batch timing is now a ROW in a small RLS-locked log table (public.sales_archive_fill_log, created in the same paste with enable RLS and revoke lines per the standing rule) and Sam reads it with a select; (b) a paste with several statements runs as one implicit transaction, so a CALL that COMMITs must be the ONLY statement in its paste, and VACUUM (run-5) stays one statement alone. (3) THE FILE: ~/Desktop/goasksam-proofs/db/run-0-scratch-proof-v2.sql, split into numbered parts, each pasted alone: PART 1 version; PART 2 setup (scratch copy of 20,000 rows, the ten columns, the log tables, two scratch procedures); PART 3 the fill, one CALL line (four batches of 5,000); PART 4 the timings grid and the progress row; PART 5 the verification grid (every _diff must be 0); PART 6 the size grid (scratch total and heap, and the real table's size now); PART 3b the COMMIT question (reset, then the COMMIT variant alone: success means run-2 is the real procedure, 'invalid transaction termination' means run-2b); PART 7 clean-up, which is also the rollback. Sam sends back the grids of parts 1, 4, 5, 6 and the outcome of 3b. The v1 file is renamed superseded-... so it cannot be run by mistake. run-3's text now reads the log table instead of notices. (4) Lane C's correction accepted with thanks: lib/onebox.js line 4861 does return setAsideRows with a reason per row on the result tier (I had read shapeCards alone); no e60 addition is needed and my earlier line to Lane C is withdrawn on that point.
